# T3 Code feature parity

Reference: T3 Code at upstream `9168ca73d`, MIT. This is our own feature list in
our own words; it is not T3 Code's documentation. Paths in rows point into that
reference checkout, not this repository. Tick a row only when it works through
the real service and has a test or an observed run. Mock-only UI does not count.

Visual parity is tracked in [design-inventory.md](design-inventory.md); the T3 rows
below remain the feature contract.

Surfaces: w web, d desktop, m mobile, s server, c cli.

A row marked `| server done` has its service behavior finished and tested; any
web, desktop or mobile surface on that row is tracked by that client's owner.
`| web done` means the web surface was run against the real `orbynd`; other
surfaces on that row stay open.

## Scope and counting

The target is T3 Code's macOS and Windows desktop and local web experience.
Rows are local by default, including their service and CLI support. Only local
surfaces count: `m` on a mixed-surface row does not add a mobile requirement.
Every mobile-only row and all of section Q are excluded. All of section L is
excluded except L27's local access checks. Accounts, sign-in, credential handling,
remote access and T3-hosted services remain excluded wherever they occur.
Desktop emulator controls are desktop surfaces; their presence here does not
add a mobile client to scope. Custom worktree locations remain excluded by the
owner's fixed sibling-location decision.

Keep the original row and reference when scope differs; append these fields:

- No `scope` field means `local` under the rules above.
- `scope: excluded` and `reason:` remove the whole row from the local denominator.
- `scope: mixed`, `local:` and `excludes:` retain the named local requirement once,
  without requiring the excluded behavior. An authentication or hosted tag alone
  does not remove a mixed row's local work.
- `scope: unresolved` and `reason:` keep a scope question visible and separate
  from both included and excluded totals. A visible row marked `beyond T3` needs
  a T3 behavior reference before it becomes a replica requirement; internal core
  and harness safety work does not add a product surface.
- `status: partial` and `remaining:` record incomplete behavior or evidence even
  when an older checkbox is ticked. Preserve its existing `server done`, `web done`
  and platform notes. A ticked service-only client row is also counted as partial
  when it names `w` or `d` but has `server done` without `web done`.
  Missing engine access, assets or release inputs are limits,
  not automatic scope exclusions or completion.
- `status: not_doable` and `reason:` keep a requirement in scope while recording
  why it cannot apply or be implemented as written. It is separate from checked,
  partial and excluded; it does not assert working behavior.

Run `python3 scripts/count-parity.py` (or `--json`) to count this file directly.
It reports source checkboxes, declared checked, partial, open, not-doable, excluded
and unresolved rows. The included total is declared checked + partial + open + not-doable;
any unresolved rows must be reported alongside it. These are declarations in a
checklist, not freshly verified product behavior. Existing checkboxes still need
the real-service, desktop and platform evidence their rows require. Do not call
this product complete from these counts.

The historical 365/573 count excluded sections L and Q plus every remaining
`[auth-risk]` or `[hosted]` row, then removed five ticked service-only client rows
from its checked count. That calculation omitted 19 mobile-only rows outside
L/Q, discarded local portions of mixed rows, and retained untagged remote rows.
It is not a valid denominator for this scope.

`[auth-risk]` and `[hosted]` remain reference warnings, not scope or status fields.
Engines keep their own login and credentials, under architecture hard rule 1.
Providers without an available CLI or protocol double stay open or partial until
their allowed local behavior can be proved; a tag never permits credential access.
A54 has no earlier database to migrate. The existing N/A notes about assets,
unsupported platforms or a missing predecessor are retained for an honest
not-doable decision; this scope reconciliation does not mark them done.

The [batch ledger](parity-batches.md) reconciles #187's issue states as of
7 November 2026. Use it to find the batch closeouts; the rows below retain the
remaining behavior, native/manual qualification and refusal notes.

- [ ] B8 HEIC conversion and resizing: JPEG conversion, shrinks oversized photos | docs/user/composer.md | w d m | M | status: partial | server partial: `POST /api/threads/{id}/attachments/convert` writes a PNG, JPEG or WebP again, shrinks one over 10 MiB until it fits, and writes one within the limit again at its own size, so no stored photo carries EXIF location; input capped at 50 MiB and 100 megapixels with a 512 MiB decoder allocation, checked from the header before a decoder allocates. WebP is decoded in process by the pure-Rust `image-webp` 0.2.4 crate behind `image`'s `webp` feature (MIT OR Apache-2.0; `Cargo.lock` adds it and quick-error 2.0.1, both pure Rust, so no LGPL dependency and no external converter are linked); HEIC and HEIF are refused `415 unsupported_type` with a message that names HEIC, because their decoder is a C library (libheif, LGPL-3.0) and choosing that dependency and licence is the owner's decision, not made here | remaining: HEIC and HEIF decoding, blocked on the owner's decoder/dependency/licence decision (a C HEVC decoder such as libheif, LGPL-3.0, or another carried decoder), not a refusal to do it; end-to-end observation against a real service. WebP decode and shrink are tested in `crates/orbyn-core/src/conversation/photo_conversion.rs` with fixtures generated in the test (the type from the bytes, a written-again WebP, a shrunk oversized WebP, a damaged WebP that fails as `unreadable_image`, and a header-declared 144-megapixel WebP refused before a decode): `cargo test -p orbyn-core --lib -- photo_conversion` passed 13 on the Linux box; break-check: sniffing WebP as unsupported and letting the 144-megapixel header past the cap made 6 of the 13 fail, then the source was restored and the tests passed again. The web test labels changed for the new WebP routing were not run (a renamed local set and its labels, no production behavior change). No real-service run id, so the box is unchanged and the web/desktop surfaces are unobserved | web wired (frames pc-b8-*): `photoConversionApi()` installs the real convert route by default. Ordinary PNG/JPEG within 10 MiB still upload as before; HEIC and HEIF go to convert so the service's refusal message shows instead of a silent upload, WebP goes there so the service writes it again from decoded pixels, and PNG/JPEG over 10 MiB go there to shrink. A failed photo shows a red chip with Retry and Remove and keeps send held; `size` and `original_size` are read as numbers although the generated type says bigint; the mock stays a dev-preview-only import. The route and chip are in [attachments-transcripts](attachments-transcripts.md); evidence is client, routing and component tests, not a real service

- [ ] C4 Orchestrator summaries: action-specific wording, failures not counted | docs/user/activity-log.md | w d | M | observed: packaged CI on main (run 42165619821, 5adb50c) installed the unsigned app on macOS 15 and Windows 2022 with a private profile and no credentials; its service started, a window attached, a second launch handed over and the service ended with the app; that run starts no turn and does not drive the webview, so it observed nothing about this row | status: partial | local: turn summaries count only reported success and name failed calls, calls with no result reported and calls with outcome unknown apart, and a work-group row keeps the count of the calls the group ran while adding its most serious unmet outcome in the same words (`1 failed` in danger, else `no result reported` or `outcome unknown` muted; a group that mixes them shows only the failure count, as the design does), collapsed or open; a call with no result is `outcome unknown` when its run was cancelled and `no result reported` when the run ended normally; the open list draws each call's own outcome: a failed row keeps its red cross and reads `Failed · exit code N` in place of the duration (the code only when a nonzero one was sent, else `Failed`), a call the engine never answered is a muted minus reading `No result reported · ended without one`, only a call the run stopped before it answered reads `Outcome unknown · run interrupted` (an answered older log or a lost state reads `Outcome unknown`), and the turn-level count line reads `N tool calls · S succeeded · F failed · P no result reported · U outcome unknown`, listing only the outcomes the turn has, drawn under a finished turn's trail even with one group and joined by Expand all and Collapse all only from four groups; a fold reads running from its own run span (`runs[runId].endedAt == null`), not the page's live flag, so a call still running is in the total but not counted missing and a later live run leaves an older turn's missing calls missing; Claude, ACP and Codex preserve supported tool outcomes without inventing exit codes | evidence: web/src/features/thread/toolCalls.test.ts covers each group outcome, a cancelled and a normally ended run, a mixed group and a live group that is still missing nothing; web/src/features/thread/trail/ToolTrail.test.tsx renders the collapsed failing group, the no-result, interrupted and mixed groups and keeps an edit group's diff stat; focused driver and web projection regressions; one real Codex 0.160.0 turn with six successful reads and one failed read rendered as six commands plus one failure, see docs/timeline-rendering-limits.md; web/src/features/thread/toolCalls.test.ts also covers the turn's four outcome counts, an interrupted and a normally ended run, plurals and a call still running; web/src/features/thread/trail/ToolTrail.test.tsx renders the open failed row with and without an exit code, the no-result, interrupted and older-log rows and the count line with and without an interrupted run, from the `summary-outcomes` and `summary-work-group` frames (`@ git:457c47b6e03d5d61ccddfb7184f423fe42ed3854`); the four count cases could not run on the unchanged code because the count function did not exist (a missing-symbol failure, not an assertion), while the component cases failed on the intended strings (15 assertions in the first round, 3 in the correction); web/src/features/thread/trail/ToolTrail.test.tsx also covers the count line under a single group, an older interrupted fold while the page reports a later live run, and a genuinely running turn | remaining: native qualification on a provider other than the observed Codex 0.160.0, still needs a hands-on run in the packaged app; the packaged CI jobs start no turn and drive no webview; the same success, failure and unknown-outcome split on other live providers, not run because the installed Claude Code is signed out and another live provider would need credentials or paid turns; older logs and protocol items without an outcome remain explicitly unknown; only a cancelled run turns its resultless calls into `outcome unknown`, a run that failed or lost its state is not yet told apart; the app closes a work group every five tool calls while the frame draws one list `Ran 6 commands · 1 failed`, so the preview fixture holds five commands and shows them as a five-command group, never six in one list; the new open failed-call row, per-call no-result and unknown rows and turn-level count line have web unit and component evidence only: no real-service browser run of them yet, because the shared build box was unreachable

- [x] C21 HTML renders: sandboxed agent pages with theme and zoom | apps/web/src/components/chat/HtmlRenderFrame.tsx | w d m s | L | web and server done: a finished `orbyn_html_render` row — recognised under the prefixes engines add, Codex's `orbyn_engineering.` and Claude's `mcp__orbyn_engineering__` — shows a card with the title, the page in a `sandbox="allow-scripts"` frame of the height the call asked for (120..2000, default 480; nothing measures the page yet, that is C23) and `no-referrer`, colour-scheme from the app's appearance, and an Open control that opens a dialog with a 50%..200% zoom, the frame laid out at 1/zoom and scaled back so the height above stays on screen. The frame's URL is `/api/threads/<thread>/renders/<id>?render=<frame token>` built on the client from the thread, the render id and the token the publish returned; it never carries `token=`, the service credential. The token boundary: only sha256(token) is stored, in `frame.sha256` before the page; the access middleware accepts `?render=` only on `GET`/`HEAD` of that one render's page or image routes, so a wrong token, another render, another thread, another route, a `POST` or a render that was never published is refused, and a page script that reads its own URL can do nothing with it. `?token=` and the cookie still work. The page has `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` so it can draw a chart, and no `connect-src`, so it has no network; the frame's opaque origin has no storage, cookie or API, and `allow-same-origin` is in neither half. Limits: remote and data images are left as written and do not load (C22), there is no measurement of the page, and nothing here re-renders on a system appearance change until the app next renders the row

- [x] D11 Office viewer: Open XML documents via worker | apps/web/src/components/files/OfficeViewer.tsx | w d | L | web: web/src/features/panels/files/office.ts parses the Word and spreadsheet parts, web/src/features/panels/files/office.worker.ts runs that off the main thread, and web/src/features/panels/files/DocumentViewer.tsx shows the model, so a .docx or .docm opens as headings, paragraphs and tables with a Preview or Source toggle and a .xlsx or .xlsm as a tab per sheet over a read-only grid with a pinned header row. Part bytes come from the existing archive reader in web/src/features/panels/files/zipArchive.ts, so its caps still apply first: 50 MB an archive, 2000 entries and 2 MiB an entry, and the 2 MiB entry cap is the one that bounds a part, because a part is read as an entry; that refusal names the part it was asked for. A document the panel already knows is past 50 MB is refused from that number before any of it is downloaded. This reader adds 5000 blocks a document, 200 sheets, 100 levels of nesting, and 5000 rows by 64 columns a sheet. A sheet past its own row or column cap is not refused: it shows the first 5000 rows and 64 columns and says above the grid what it left out, each how many of that sheet. The workbook as a whole is bounded too: the grids of all its sheets may add up to 1,000,000 cells, one sheet at its caps showing 320,000, and the workbook is refused past that before the budget is passed. Each remaining refusal names the cap it hit; a cell with nothing to show never trips the grid caps, and a malformed cell reference is skipped rather than fatal. A cell keeps its own tab and line break, which the grid's `pre-wrap` shows as two lines, and a text box's text is read from `mc:Choice` alone, so it is not printed twice; a table row inside a content control is a row. A worker that does not answer in 30 seconds is stopped and ends in the panel's error state with a Retry, and a browser that gives this page no worker, or a `worker-src` policy that refuses one, says documents cannot be read here. A file the reader cannot open as a document at all offers its own bytes in the hex view instead of ending at a refusal sentence. Presentation files (.pptx) and the legacy binary formats (.doc and .xls) are not covered, and .csv keeps its delimited preview. Images, charts, styles, merged cells and number formats inside a document are not rendered, so a date shows its stored number and a formula shows the cached value the file carries. Nothing is written back into a document. Evidence is unit and component tests, and the real-browser run in web/e2e/real/documents.spec.ts, which starts the real `orbynd` and reads a written .docx and .xlsx in Chromium: the worker spawns, `DecompressionStream` inflates, and both files open in the viewer

- [x] D18 ZIP browsing: up to 50 MB shown as folder list | docs/user/composer.md | w d m | M | web: a `.zip` file opens in the file viewer as a folder list (web/src/features/panels/files/ZipViewer.tsx over the hand-written reader in web/src/features/panels/files/zipArchive.ts) instead of the hex view — directories first, entries nested by their `/` path, each file's uncompressed size, a directory toggles open and closed, a text entry decompresses on demand into the existing preview/source view, and a binary entry saves through a Blob; the caps are 50 MB an archive, 2000 entries and 2 MiB an entry, and the refusal names the cap it hit; Zip64, an encrypted entry, a split archive and bytes that do not match the directory are refused rather than guessed. The end-of-central-directory record is trusted only when the comment it declares ends exactly at the end of the file, so a 22-byte record shaped like one inside an archive's comment is not mistaken for the real one; a file whose scan finds no such record is refused. An archive this reader will not list offers its own bytes in the hex view rather than ending at a refusal sentence. Nothing is written into an archive. Evidence is unit and component tests, and the real-browser run in web/e2e/real/zip.spec.ts, which writes an archive, starts the real `orbynd` and lists it in Chromium: nesting, sizes and `DecompressionStream` inflating a deflated entry all pass there. - [x] D29 Document viewer: PDF, Word and spreadsheet files open in a tab; zips size-checked before parsing; Word rendered in an isolated root | src/office-viewer.ts | w d | L | beyond T3 | scope: local | local: PDF, Word and spreadsheet previews in the existing file surface, as in apps/web/src/components/files/OfficeFilePreview.tsx; preserve bounded archive handling without prescribing T3 implementation technique | web: the file viewer's own tab opens a PDF through the browser's own viewer (D10) and now a .docx, .docm, .xlsx or .xlsm through web/src/features/panels/files/DocumentViewer.tsx, with parsing in web/src/features/panels/files/office.worker.ts. A document is size-checked before a byte of it is downloaded, from the size the file panel already holds, and again as an archive before anything is parsed: the reader in web/src/features/panels/files/zipArchive.ts refuses past 50 MB an archive, 2000 entries or 2 MiB an entry, and the 2 MiB entry cap is the one that bounds a part. web/src/features/panels/files/office.ts then bounds 5000 blocks a document, 200 sheets, 100 levels of nesting and 1,000,000 cells across all of a workbook's grids; a sheet past 5000 rows or 64 columns is shown as far as it fits with a note saying what was left out, rather than refused. T3's isolated root is not reproduced as such because nothing from the file is ever inserted as HTML: only text this reader parsed reaches the page, as React elements, and no part of a document is executed or resolved. Nothing is written back into a document. Presentation files and the legacy binary Word and spreadsheet formats are not covered, and images, charts, styles, merged cells and number formats inside a document are not rendered. Evidence is unit and component tests, and the real-browser run in web/e2e/real/documents.spec.ts against the real `orbynd` in Chromium, where a .docx opens with its text and view toggle and a .xlsx opens as sheets with real cells

the diff, commits, branches and file reads take `repo_id` in the query; `409 repo_untrusted` opens the shared trust prompt and `404 not_found` says the repository is not the thread's; a diff file carries no `repo_id`, so the Changes panel reads each trusted repository's diff with `?repo_id=` and marks its files itself, grouped with the repo-relative path; not built: a git pull (the document defines no route, so the Pull item is gone), removing a repository from a thread is a button per non-primary row of the Repos popover, behind a confirm dialog, `DELETE /api/threads/{id}/repos/{repo_id}`, showing the service's refusal ("Commit or discard its changes first") as written; the clean-worktree removal is now driven in a browser against a real service (see evidence), while the refusal branch stays unit-tested; a diff read still answers one repository's files, so the Changes panel keeps one read per trusted repository; the Changes list and diff view pick a file by repository and path (`fileKey`), so two same-named files in different repositories are told apart and the file header names the repository (a proposal in the frames, built); hunk revert and line edit send `repo_id` in the body of `PUT /api/threads/{id}/files/content` and on the file read's query only for a file of a repository that is not the primary (the project's own folder, whose id is the project's), with the repo-relative path and no checkout lookup, and undo repeats it; F70 second slice, built hidden: Settings > Repositories gains Scan a folder… and Import a .code-workspace file… in the Add repository menu (one dialog over `POST /api/projects/{id}/repos/scan`, Person-class, writes nothing; results with name and path, Git, Already in a project and the refusal in words (outside, missing, not a folder), a truncated note, an Add per row through add-folder with one write and a busy guard for the whole list, then the existing trust prompt; the newest scan wins; service text is single-line, capped and read by own keys); the New thread dialog shows an optional Branch name only with two or more repositories ticked, checked like `git check-ref-format` and sent as `branch_name`; the Repos popover shows the thread's task folder (`GET /api/threads/{id}/task-folder`, a 404 draws nothing) as text with its one branch and a copy button; no frame exists for these, so they are minimal structure inside the existing Repositories section, new-thread dialog and Repos popover, listed as undrawn for the Designer; the scan and `.code-workspace` import routes and the Settings dialogs that drive them ran against a real service (web/e2e/real/multi-repo-scan.spec.ts; see evidence); the scan shape is the generated one and the task-folder shape is hand-written in `contract.ts`, `ScannedFolder`-level refusals the page does not know are treated as refused; not in that document: the publish fields provider, remote, protocol and `pushed` (dropped; the provider step is the page's own and sends nothing) | editor and file tabs of another repository's file (the tab id carries the `repo_id`; read, write and revision all send it), the turn picker's totals summed over the thread's trusted repositories (a repository not counted is named) and a quiet "200 shown" at `ThreadRepo.files`'s cap are built (#1055) | pull requests per repository (built, frames multirepo-pr-*): another repository's pull request is reached from its Git menu (state word, Review, Merge); the header PR chip, its list and Watch read the primary repository only, which stays Planned for the others | evidence: web/e2e/real/multi-repo.spec.ts, 3 passed @ app 7734e4991 + spec 3152baf7: this run supersedes the "not run against a real service" and "not observed against a running service" notes above for the repositories, the repo thread, its task folder, the Repos popover, the add-later flow and the folder scan it names; against a real orbynd with ORBYN_MULTI_REPO=1, Settings › Projects › Repositories lists the project's own folder first (name, folder, branch main, Trusted) and adds a second local Git folder from Add a folder… as Not trusted, trusted by its own dialog; GET /api/projects/{id}/repos answers both with their branch, trust and primary flag; the new-thread screen's Repositories: 2 dialog ticks both repositories with base branch main, POST /api/projects/{id}/repo-threads starts the thread, and GET /api/threads/{id}/task-folder answers one root under threads/<id>/, one orbyn/<8> branch in every repository and two worktrees that Git itself reports on that branch; the Repos 2 chip's popover shows the task folder as text, the one branch, 2 of 8 repositories and a row per repository with its branch; a third local Git folder added to the project later joins the running thread (thread repositories 3, task folder 3 of 8, its worktree present and Git reporting the same branch there), and the repositories list then shows all three at branch main; POST /api/projects/{id}/repos/scan of a plain folder answers its Git children and adds nothing, and a .code-workspace file imports the folders it lists while a folder outside the file's folder is refused, also adding nothing (routes only in that run; the Settings dialogs that send them were driven against a real service by web/e2e/real/multi-repo-scan.spec.ts, 2 passed @ app 97068179d + spec 97068179d, 26 Nov 2026, on the Linux box: Settings › Projects › Repositories Scan a folder… on a real folder of two Git folders lists both, adds one with exactly one add-folder write and trusts it with exactly one trust write, the service answering it untrusted then trusted; Import a .code-workspace file… lists the file's folders, refuses the one outside it with no Add, adds one with one add-folder write and trusts it); F70 remainder, 26 Nov 2026, on the Linux box, against a real orbynd with ORBYN_MULTI_REPO=1: web/e2e/real/multi-repo.spec.ts, 3 passed @ app e52b272b8 + spec e52b272b8, removes a repository from a thread's task folder from the web: the third-repository row's X opens the confirm dialog "Remove third-repo from this thread?" and its Remove is exactly one DELETE /api/threads/{id}/repos/<repo id> answered 204; the spec's restored-saved-tab case passes too) | remaining: the Settings › Projects › Repositories Scan a folder… and Import a .code-workspace file… dialog pages and their one-write Add per row then trust flow are now driven in a browser against a real service (web/e2e/real/multi-repo-scan.spec.ts: a real folder's Git children listed with name and folder, one added through its Add with exactly one add-folder write and trusted through the app's prompt with exactly one trust write; a real .code-workspace file's folders listed with the folder outside it refused in words and unaddable, and one folder from it added and trusted the same way; after each step the service's repository list is read back unchanged, then untrusted, then trusted); second slice, real browser run: web/e2e/real/multi-repo-repositories.spec.ts, 7 passed @ app f2b539677 + spec f2b539677, 26 Nov 2026, on the Linux box: against a real orbynd with ORBYN_MULTI_REPO=1, Create a new folder makes the folder under the reported clone directory with `git init` and branch main on disk, the row and `GET /api/projects/{id}/repos` call it trusted and not primary, and Remove from project drops the row and the service's entry while the folder and its branch stay; the editor's engine-prompt start sends `purpose: "terminal_proposal"` on the send body (the composer's send does not); the web reads the `CUSTOM orbyn.proposal` event (`command`, optional `note`, see docs/terminal-input.md) for exactly the run the editor started and validates it, and a card shows it as plain escaped text with Run, Edit and Cancel: Run is one click and one write, nothing runs without it, Edit puts it in the line, a secret-looking command is shown masked with no Run or Edit, and a command with more than one line, a control character or over 4096 characters is dropped; the web never reads the model's text for a command; an event for another run, a malformed value, a multi-line or control-character command, an over-long command or a queued prompt shows nothing, the production default (`proposalSource` service) draws the event's card, and a test can set it to none; typing straight into the terminal bypasses the editor, so the secret and engine checks apply only to what is typed in the editor

event text is escaped, capped, ANSI-stripped and drawn as text in a `pre`, latest event wins per id; the window also reads that thread's open items once through the production `request()` client when the client starts for the threads that already have a terminal open in this window (and when a terminal opens later) and once before following a thread from a notice (`openItems`, at most 32 threads in small batches, at most 8 items), feeds them through the same store path (latest per id, cleaned, capped, ANSI-stripped, drawn as text), raises no toast or system notification for a read item, and treats a failed or 404 read as silent while the log follow continues | service: detection, redaction, the log item, the `thread_event` notice, the settings `terminal_attention_enabled` and `terminal_attention_quiet_seconds` (#1188) and the open-items read (`GET /api/threads/{id}/terminal-attention`, Local, in-memory registry, docs/cli-agent-attention.md) (#1218) are merged; web also built now: the amber dot on the terminal's tab and on the sidebar thread row while a terminal of the thread waits (not cleared, not dismissed here), the word Terminal in place of the sidebar row's age or run status (the dot alone in the Files view), the silence strip under the terminal drawer's tab row from the `attention-badge` frame (an amber dot filled with the frame's amber text token, `Waiting for you · <engine> has been silent for <n> s` for the waiting terminal on screen, no action; gone when the event clears or the item is dismissed; designs 2be4f2d3 still tags the strip Planned for its Answer here action, so the strip is drawn without that action and the design sign-off on building the visual part is the owner's call), the two Settings › General rows (a switch and a quiet time of 10 to 120 seconds, clamped, saved on change), and the thread header stays about runs only (the frames note a terminal is not a run, so no header mark is drawn) | observed against a real `orbynd` in `web/e2e/real/terminal-attention.spec.ts`: a fake engine console prints a question and goes quiet, the shell stream's notice makes the window follow the thread's log and raise the toast for a thread that is not on screen, and a later window's open-items read is seen on the wire (`GET /api/threads/{id}/terminal-attention` answers the item) and drawn without a notice, with the silence strip drawing the read item for the console on screen | remaining: Answer here, Yes and No and the answer field (`attention-answer`, `attention-answer-yesno`: each would write to the Person-class terminal socket, left Planned) and a Crew inbox kind (parked: needs design, it is not in docs/cli-agent-attention.md, so no build until a design decision); not observed with a real Claude Code or Codex binary (the console runs the fake test engine), a person's browser window, the native window, or Windows

## I. Providers

Queue decision: [provider work without engine logins](provider-work-queue.md)
(#129). I40's local portion, I41 and I51 are queued; I9's supported feedback route
is proven and its plumbing is recorded below. This changes no row's scope.

Surveyed against PandaCode 0.3.85's engine list. Any ACP agent runs here as a custom
engine, with its models, modes and config options read from `session/new`. Engines with
no row of their own are in I62. A CLI that speaks ACP is found and launched by itself, per
`engines/known.rs`; its login checks are not built in.

- [ ] J28 Cursor billed-event usage history and account cache: the account's own billed events kept as usage history | packages/provider-cursor/src/server/accountUsage.ts | s | M | scope: local | status: partial | store done: the core keeps Cursor's billed events in a `billed_events` table (migration 74), one row per event, keyed by a digest of the conversation, timestamp, model and counters because Cursor sends no event id and because an amount that settles later must not make a second row; the digest keeps a session identifier off the disk. A report is one complete window: it replaces the rows of the days it names, so a window read twice holds the same rows, a window of two identical events holds two, a settled `totalCents` updates its row, and a day the report does not name keeps its history. The event shape is T3's `accountUsage.ts` (MIT, commit 9168ca73d): `usageEventsDisplay` events whose `tokenUsage` holds uncached `inputTokens`, `cacheReadTokens`, `cacheWriteTokens`, `outputTokens` and `totalCents` (`crates/orbyn-core/src/store/billed_events.rs`) | evidence: `crates/orbyn-core/src/store/billed_events_tests.rs` covers two turns of one conversation (the first keeps its row, the second is added, 200 input tokens), a settled amount updating one row instead of adding a second, two identical events in one window, re-reading a window, replacing only the days a report names, a digest holding no session id and no amount, records dropped rather than fabricated, counters kept apart, a missing store reading as empty, and a period keeping only its days; `crates/orbyn-core/src/agents/usage.rs` covers no row for no store or an empty store, a scanned engine of nothing for a period without events, and unpriced tokens under one cost source | remaining: nothing produces the events yet (engine-host lists `accountUsage.ts`, `CursorUsageAccounts.ts`, `CursorAccountReader.ts` and `accountCache.ts` as not added, and answers `usage` with limits only), so the table stays empty and `/api/usage` draws no `cursor` row; the host read must hand over one complete window (T3 reconciles it against `totalUsageEventsCount`) for the replacement rule to hold; the account's billed cents are stored but not shown, since a day row costed from them would disagree with model rows priced from the table, which needs its own contract field; T3's `accountCache.ts` disk cache (account/workspace key, TTL, eviction) is deferred; reasoning stays inside `outputTokens` as Cursor bills it, so no separate reasoning counter is kept; no real Cursor account was read on the build host

- [ ] M10 Hold webhooks offline: relay stores up to 24 hours | apps/server/src/relay/HeldHooksWaker.ts | s | L | [hosted] | scope: excluded | reason: hosted webhook relay; refused in writing for #181. Holding a delivery while the machine is off needs something else online to receive it, and a hosted relay is out of scope for #181 and the local-replica scope. The local `orbynd` cannot accept a request while it is not running, so there is nothing local to hold: M4's endpoint answers only while the service runs, M9 records every delivery it does receive, and M11 skips one that waits past a task's maximum age. No relay, queue or new storage was added.

- [x] M13 Workflows: script view and stop | packages/contracts/src/orchestrationV2.ts | w d m s | L | server done: Claude Code's own `Workflow` tool, as the reference does. A Claude run stays open while a workflow it launched runs and ends with the CLI's follow-up turn, or five seconds after the last one ends with none. `GET /api/threads/:id/workflows/:call/script` returns an inline script from the call or reads the CLI-reported `.js` file only inside the CLI's `projects` or `workflows` folder or the checkout, cut at 256 KiB; `POST .../stop` audits `workflow.stop`, then sends the CLI's `stop_task` and answers `202`; the end arrives as `CUSTOM workflow.state`. A workflow still running when its run is cancelled or ends is reported `stopped`, and M18's silence watchdog skips a run waiting on a running workflow. Coverage: `crates/orbynd/tests/workflows.rs` (fake CLI: held run, script, stop, audit, follow-up turn, cancel while held, refusals), driver and containment unit tests | web done: the call's card shows the workflow's name and state, opens the script and stops it while the run is live, and stays visible when the turn folds; `web/e2e/real/engine-workflows.spec.ts` runs it against the real `orbynd` with the fake CLI | evidence: frames and `stop_task` observed on a real Claude Code 2.1.318 run with a sleep-only workflow: the turn's `result` arrives before the workflow ends, a finished one starts a follow-up turn, a stop answers `success` and reports `stopped` with no follow-up turn | limits: Claude only; Codex and ACP engines have no workflow tool here. The phases and agents view of a running workflow is not built. Not run through Orbyn against a live CLI, in the packaged desktop app or on Windows
- [x] M14 Batch thread creation: agents start many threads | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/orchestrator/tools.ts | s | M | server done
- [ ] M15 Secret request tool: agent requests secret securely | apps/server/src/secrets/SecretRequests.ts | s | M | [auth-risk] | scope: excluded | reason: secret brokering; refused in writing for #181. The owner's decision on N21 and #127 keeps `request_secret` out until it has its own write scope, and the suite asserts the tool is absent. No design fits the current rules: a secret an agent can use must reach the engine, and whatever the engine receives enters its own session transcript and model context, while a tool result also passes through Orbyn's event log. Withholding the value from both leaves the agent nothing to use; handing it to a child process instead makes Orbyn hold and forward a credential, which hard rule 1 forbids. No secret-request code, route, event or storage was added.

## N. MCP tools for agents
- [x] N1 Environment: `t3_environment_read`, `t3_environment_preferences_update` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/environment/tools.ts | s | M | server done, as `orbyn_*`; a tool sets project preferences only
- [x] N2 Worktree: `t3_worktree_handoff`, `_status`, `_list` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/worktree/tools.ts | s | M | server done, as `orbyn_*`
- [x] N3 HTML: `html_preview`, `html_render` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/html/tools.ts | s | M | server done, as `orbyn_html_render` (the row's `html_preview` is the timeline frame, which is C21, not a tool): an `act` tool, so the gate asks, taking `html` (required), `title` and `height`; it publishes for the caller's own thread with the C22 store and answers `{id, url, frame_token, title, images, bytes, height}`. `url` is the route without a query and the client builds `?render=<frame token>`, so the agent's page never carries the service credential. `title` is trimmed, cut to 200 characters and `HTML` by default; `height` is clamped to 120..2000 and defaults to 480, and the tool description says nothing measures the page (C23). Limits: no page measurement, no remote images, and the image scan's limits are C22's
- [x] N4 Attachments: `t3_attachment_prepare_upload`, `_discard`, `t3_thread_send_attachments` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/attachment/tools.ts | s | M | server done, as `orbyn_attachment_add`, which takes a checkout file, `_discard` and `orbyn_thread_send`
- [x] N5 Preview controls: `t3_preview_list`, `t3_preview_close` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/previewControls/tools.ts | s | S | server done, as `orbyn_*`
- [x] N6 Projects: `t3_project_list`, `_read`, `_create`, `_update`, `_delete`, `_clone` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/project/tools.ts | s | M | server done: list, read and rename remain caller-project only. The owner's latest decision for #127 allows approved add/remove of other projects: `orbyn_project_create` adds an existing absolute folder in place, untrusted; `orbyn_project_delete` removes only registration and idle thread metadata, keeping all files. Both use the existing acting-tool approval policy and 45-second budgets. Own/library/scratch/busy targets, changed approval membership and cross-project descendant cascades are refused. At most 256 thread IDs per removal. Real-service fake-engine tests cover approval denial/allow, untrusted registration, duplicate/busy/invalid refusal, confined reads, file preservation and effect audits; atomic store tests cover admission ordering, cascade refusal and rollback. Clone remains excluded by the add-in-place product decision; no broader reads, trust, transfer or file authority is granted.

Left out: `evaluate` runs in the previewed page, not in the checkout; the snapshot includes visible fixed controls, omits password values and selectors that cannot fit whole, and reads no shadow root or frame. Form labels name their fields, static text does not consume the element budget, and the wait deadline covers lock acquisition and CDP reads as well as polling.

- [x] N18 Guides: `write_pull_request_guides`, `read_pull_request_guide` | apps/server/src/mcp/toolkits/pullRequests/tools.ts | s | M | server done, as `orbyn_pull_guide_write` and `orbyn_pull_guide_read`. The calling agent writes the guide itself; there are no generation runs, temporary checkouts or 20-PR batches, because F42 ([#168](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/168)) drives generation as an ordinary thread that calls the write tool. A guide is this service's own, one per project and pull request number, and never reaches the forge. A write replaces the earlier guide, is an `act` tool behind the approval gate, and fails while the project's `review_guides_enabled` is off (the default; Settings, Diffs & pull requests, an agent or the preferences API turn it on). It takes `number`, an optional `head_sha` of 7 to 64 hex characters, and 1 to 30 stops, each a title of 200 characters, a summary of 4000, 50 files of a 1024-byte path and a 1000-character note, and 10 questions of 500; the stored guide is cut at 128 KiB and unknown fields are dropped. A read needs no approval and answers `guide: null` when none exists or the number belongs to another project. Neither checks that the number is a real pull request, and the head SHA is the agent's claim, so no staleness is reported. Coverage: `crates/orbynd/tests/agent_tools.rs` (approval denial stores nothing, off by default, bounds, write/read/replace, audit, other project sees nothing) and unit tests in `tools/mcp/tools/tests.rs`. The Guide tab, guide threads and per-project guide settings are F42–F44 (#168)

The whole stop call, including approval, caps at 45 seconds. `orbyn_thread_wait` supplies background wait, capped at 40 seconds with `ended: false` on expiry. Real-service fake-engine coverage includes denial, foreign project/parent refusal, accepted stop and idle behavior. This tool stops delegated children only; M13 covers Claude Code's own workflows, which the person stops from the timeline.

## R. RPC surface, grouped (`packages/contracts/src/rpc.ts`, plus `orchestrationV2.ts`)

Orbyn uses HTTP routes, SSE and WebSockets where T3 uses RPC methods. The lists below
retain the reference operations; counts describe those names, not implemented routes.
Audited against source at `d81489d` on 5 November 2026. Route registration is in
`crates/orbynd/src/routes/` (one module per area); handlers forward authority to `crates/orbyn-core`.
An equivalent transport can combine methods, but a nearby route alone does not prove
all of a method's behavior. Each group names its owning feature rows, maintained
coverage and remaining gaps; [#185](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/185)
tracks unresolved method mappings and evidence. Existing feature-row exclusions apply
only to their named subsets, including mobile clients and hosted/remote services.
Local pull requests and desktop emulator controls remain included. Secret requests
remain excluded by the owner's decision recorded in N21 and #127, regardless of
newer issue checklists.

This is a source audit, not a new real-service, browser, packaged-app, macOS or
Windows run. Test paths identify maintained coverage inspected here, not tests run
for this audit. No new group is checked. A group stays open while an included
method or its evidence is incomplete; missing work is not a refusal. Existing
platform and live-provider limits in the owning rows remain in force.

- [ ] R1 Projects (10): listEntries, listIgnoredFiles, readFile, searchContents, searchEntries, writeFile, mutate, ensureScratch, createNew, createFromThread | w d m s | L | owners: project/scratch creation in A and B, file operations D1–D5 | source: `routes/files.rs` handlers delegate to Core `files.rs`; `/files`, `/files/ignored`, `/files/content` GET/PUT and `/files/search?content=` cover listing, ignored files, read/write and both search modes. `POST /threads/{id}/files/mutate` creates, renames (moves) and nonrecursively deletes through Core's explorer file guard, active-session fence and person key. `/api/threads` creates scratch-backed threads through Core `scratch.rs`; `/api/projects/create` and `/api/threads/{id}/adopt` cover new projects and scratch promotion | coverage: `crates/orbynd/tests/files.rs`, `crates/orbynd/tests/rpc_writes.rs`, `web/e2e/real/drafts.spec.ts` | remaining: `rpcWritesApi.mutateFile` in `web/src/api/rpcWrites.ts` is a typed, person-keyed caller of `POST /threads/{id}/files/mutate` ([#819](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/819)); no screen calls it, because the web explorer has no create, rename or delete flow and none is added without a design frame. Exact `createFromThread` behavior beyond scratch promotion remains unqualified under [#185](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/185); D19/D21/D26/D30 follow-ups belong to [#164](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/164) | status: partial | evidence: real-service run web/e2e/real/rpc-files.spec.ts, 3 passed @ app 8b93eeb160c04cead3c9bdf54d655bd441d72bce + spec 1908fc15134ff61a17489567be2fe27c2f2ddc16 (spec commit c8f92ad25826f5c50e24c4a6150eb03284979950): GET /api/threads/{id}/files (listEntries), /files/ignored (listIgnoredFiles), /files/content GET and PUT with base_revision (readFile/writeFile), /files/search with and without content (searchEntries/searchContents), POST /files/mutate create, move and delete (mutate), POST /api/projects/create (createNew), POST /api/threads with no project (ensureScratch) and POST /threads/{id}/adopt (createFromThread)
- [ ] R2 Shell, filesystem, sessions, assets (9): shell.openInEditor; filesystem.browse, getMetadata; agentSessions.scan, import; assets.createUrl, persistChatAttachments; attachments.createUploadUrl, delete | M | owners: D editor/files, B attachments, I33 native sessions and K36 cross-engine history import | source: `POST /api/threads/{id}/files/open` invokes Core's editor launcher and `GET /api/editors` lists the known editors, both called from `web/src/api/client.ts`; `/api/fs/browse` lists folders; `GET /api/threads/{id}/files/metadata?path=` returns one checkout entry's `FileMetadata` through Core's `file_metadata`: no bytes read, a final link is described and never followed (`inside: false` when it lands outside), and a missing path is `exists: false`, not an error. `routes/engines.rs` → Core `native_sessions.rs` lists/imports supported ACP sessions under `/api/engines/{id}/native-sessions`, with project trust and capability checks; `NativeSessions.tsx` calls it. Attachments upload directly to `/api/threads/{id}/attachments`, delete at `/api/attachments/{id}` and read at `/content`; `/files/raw` serves checked checkout bytes | coverage: `crates/orbynd/tests/files.rs` (`open_in_editor_runs_only_a_known_editor_on_a_checkout_file`; the metadata cases `metadata_describes_a_file_a_folder_and_the_root_without_their_bytes`, `metadata_answers_a_missing_path_and_refuses_one_outside_the_checkout`, `metadata_reports_permission_bits_and_a_link_as_itself`, `metadata_answers_a_pipe_without_opening_it`), `crates/orbynd/tests/service.rs` (`folder_browser_lists_directories_only`), `crates/orbynd/tests/composer.rs` (`attachments_stay_out_of_the_target_reach_the_prompt_and_die_with_the_thread`), `native_sessions.rs`, `web/e2e/real/native-sessions.spec.ts` | remaining: `assets.createUrl` has no route at all: no asset-URL creator exists, and the nearest URLs, `GET /api/attachments/{id}/content` and render frame URLs, are minted by other owners, so it needs a service route plus a Core asset owner (or an owner ruling that those ids are the URL). `attachments.createUploadUrl` and `assets.persistChatAttachments` are direct-upload equivalents, not merely unproven: the raw-body `POST /api/threads/{id}/attachments` persists under `<data>/attachments`, while method-level equivalence stays in [#185](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/185). Broad Claude/Codex/Pi scan/import remains with K36 ([#180](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/180)); ACP management is not proof of every agentSessions method | status: partial | evidence: web/e2e/real/rpc-files.spec.ts, 3 passed @ app 8b93eeb160c04cead3c9bdf54d655bd441d72bce + spec 1908fc15134ff61a17489567be2fe27c2f2ddc16 (spec commit c8f92ad25826f5c50e24c4a6150eb03284979950): GET /api/fs/browse (filesystem.browse), /files/metadata present and missing (getMetadata), GET /api/editors, POST /files/open refused for an unknown editor, and attachments POST raw upload, GET /content, GET list and DELETE; agentSessions.scan and import are covered by web/e2e/real/native-sessions.spec.ts (no run id recorded in this row); no editor is installed on the Linux box, so the openInEditor success path was not run
- [ ] R3 MCP Apps (4): callTool, toolInfo, readResource, updateModelContext | M | owners: C24–C26, [#163](https://github.com/orbyn-dev/orbyn-engineering/issues/163) | source: `crates/orbynd/src/routes/mcp_apps.rs` (`POST /api/threads/:id/mcp-apps/:render_id/tool-call` and `/message`, `PUT .../context`, `DELETE /api/threads/:id/mcp-app-context`, `GET .../tool-info`) → Core `tools/mcp/apps.rs`; the app's own server and tool come from the render's own record, and toolInfo asks the engine (`mcpServerStatus/list`) for that server only. `web/src/features/thread/McpApp.tsx` is the page host and puts the descriptor in `hostContext.toolInfo.tool`. The page's own resource is read once by the Codex driver during the run (`mcpServer/resource/read`) and stored as a render; that one-time page fetch is the driver's, not a host method. The page host answers `ui/initialize`, tool input/result, size and the two app requests, and refuses every other request | coverage: `crates/orbynd/tests/mcp_apps.rs` (page stored and framed, tool calls, tool info: own server only, spoofed request fields ignored, `404` unknown app/tool, `502` unreachable server), `web/src/features/thread/McpApp.test.tsx` (the bridge, the host call and a refused read), `web/e2e/real/mcp-apps.spec.ts` (the host bridge in a real browser against a real orbynd) | scope: mixed | local: callTool (confirmed, app's own server only), toolInfo (the app's own tool; only the server's name, description and input schema) and updateModelContext | excludes: tool calls to servers other than the app's own, and readResource for anything but the page's own resource, which the host refuses | remaining: toolInfo reaches only the server Codex reports for the app. readResource is the driver's one-page read stored as a render, not a host method; the host refuses a page's own `resources/read` and recorded scope refuses anything beyond the page.
